How is the per-trial `neural` data aligned to the event described in the `instructions`?\n\ni. Spike times and event times are already on the same session-absolute clock, so no alignment step is needed. The bin edges relative to the go cue are added to each trial's go-cue time to give the absolute time window for that trial, and the spikes are binned against those edges directly.\n\nii.\n```python\ngo = np.asarray(bev['go_start_times'].timestamps)\n```\n\n```python\nedges = (go[:, None] + REL_EDGES[None, :]).ravel()\nrates = np.empty((good.size, n_trials, N_BINS), np.float32)\nfor r, u in enumerate(good):\n    s = allst[starts[u]:offs[u]]\n    pos = np.searchsorted(s, edges).reshape(n_trials, N_BINS + 1)\n    rates[r] = np.diff(pos, axis=1)\n```\n\niii. Everything in the NWB file is timestamped on one global clock, so aligning to the go cue only requires looking up each trial's go-cue time and taking the window around it. There is no resampling or interpolation, and no per-stream offset to correct.\n\n## 2-e. How is the `neural` data temporally binned/resampled?\n\ni. Spike times are binned into 80 non-overlapping 50 ms bins spanning -2.5 s to +1.5 s relative to the go cue. The bin grid is defined once, as 81 edges relative to the go cue, and reused for every trial and session, so every trial has the same 80 timepoints.\n\nii.\n```python\n# trial window relative to the go cue, and bin width, in seconds\nT_START, T_STOP = -2.5, 1.5\nBIN = 0.05\nN_BINS = int(round((T_STOP - T_START) / BIN))             \n\nREL_EDGES = T_START + BIN * np.arange(N_BINS + 1)         \nCENTERS = REL_EDGES[:-1] + BIN / 2                        \n```\n\n```python\nedges = (go[:, None] + REL_EDGES[None, :]).ravel()\n```\n\niii. The window and the 50 ms bin width are set by the instructions. Defining the grid once as offsets from the go cue gives a constant 80 timepoints per trial, which the target format requires.\n\n## 3-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *time_from_tone_onset* derived from?\n\ni. From `sample_start_times`, the tone onsets of the session, together with the go cue of each trial. The tone taken for a trial is the **last one** before its go cue.\n\nii. The tone of each trial:\n```python\nsample = np.asarray(bev['sample_start_times'].timestamps)\ntone = sample[np.searchsorted(sample, go, side='left') - 1]\n```\n\niii. An early lick replays the sample epoch, so a trial can carry more than one tone; the last one before the go cue is the one the animal actually used.\n\n## 3-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *time_from_tone_onset*?\n\ni. The bins sit around the go cue, so the value of a bin is its center plus the gap between the tone and the go cue.\n\nii. The bin center, and the shift that turns them into time from the tone:\n```python\nCENTERS = REL_EDGES[:-1] + BIN / 2                        \n```\n```python\n# bins sit around the go cue, values are seconds since the tone\ntime_from_tone = CENTERS[None, :] + (go - tone)[:, None]\n```\n\niii. No additional processing needed other than find the tone onset time.\n\n## 3-c. How is `input` *time_from_tone_onset* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. It is how the neural binnning grid is defined.\n\nii. The grid, defined once as offsets from the go cue, and laid on each trial's go cue to bin the spikes:\n```python\nT_START, T_STOP = -2.5, 1.5\nBIN = 0.05\nN_BINS = int(round((T_STOP - T_START) / BIN))             \n\nREL_EDGES = T_START + BIN * np.arange(N_BINS + 1)         \nCENTERS = REL_EDGES[:-1] + BIN / 2                        \n```\n```python\nedges = (go[:, None] + REL_EDGES[None, :]).ravel()\n```\n\niii. N/A\n\n## 4-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *photostim* derived from?\n\ni. From `photostim_onset` and `photostim_duration` in the trials table, with `start_time` and the go cue used to place them on the trial's time axis.\n\nii. The onset and offset of the stimulation, relative to the go cue:\n```python\non_s = trials['photostim_onset'].values\nhas = on_s != 'N/A'\nstim_on[has] = trials['start_time'].values[has] + on_s[has].astype(float) - go[has]\nstim_off[has] = stim_on[has] + trials['photostim_duration'].values[has].astype(float)\n```\n\niii. The onsets are stored as strings measured from trial start, with `'N/A'` on the trials that were not stimulated, so they have to be converted and re-expressed against the go cue the bins are aligned on.\n\n## 4-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *photostim*?\n\ni. A bin is 1 where its center falls between the onset and the offset of the stimulation and 0 elsewhere, so the input is a binary time series rather than a per trial flag.\n\nii. The bins the light is on for:\n```python\n# NaN compares False, so non-stim trials stay 0\nphotostim = ((CENTERS[None, :] >= stim_on[:, None])\n             & (CENTERS[None, :] < stim_off[:, None]))\n```\n\niii. A trial without stimulation keeps NaN bounds, and NaN compares false, so all of its bins come out 0 without a separate branch.\n\n## 4-c. How is `input` *photostim* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. The onset and offset are expressed relative to the go cue, which is the event the neural bins are aligned on, so they can be compared against the bin center directly.\n\nii. The onset put on the same axis as the bins:\n```python\nstim_on[has] = trials['start_time'].values[has] + on_s[has].astype(float) - go[has]\n```\n\niii. N/A\n\n## 5-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *choice* derived from?\n\ni. There is no choice column in the file. Choice is derived from two trials-table columns: `trial_instruction` (`'left'` / `'right'`, the side the tone instructed) and `outcome` (`'hit'` / `'miss'` / `'ignore'`). A hit means the animal licked the instructed side, a miss means it licked the other side, and an `ignore` means it never licked.\n\nii.\n```python\nSIDE_CODE = {'left': 0, 'right': 1}\nCHOICE_NO_LICK = 2               # no lick in the response window\n```\n\n```python\n# choice is not stored; derive it from instruction x outcome\noutcome_s = trials['outcome'].values\nside = np.array([SIDE_CODE[x] for x in trials['trial_instruction'].values])\nchoice = np.where(outcome_s == 'ignore', CHOICE_NO_LICK,\n                  np.where(outcome_s == 'hit', side, 1 - side))\n```\n\niii. The animal's actual lick direction is not stored, but it is fully determined by the instructed side and the outcome, so it is derived from those two columns. `outcome == 'ignore'` was verified to mean no lick anywhere in `[go, go + 1.5]`, with no exceptions, so choice is genuinely undefined on those trials and gets its own third value.\n\n## 5-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *choice*?\n\ni. The derived choice is coded as `0` left, `1` right, `2` no lick, and written into row 0 of the per-trial output array, repeated across all 80 bins. `output_values[0]` names the three codes.\n\nii.\n```python\nSIDE_CODE = {'left': 0, 'right': 1}\nCHOICE_NO_LICK = 2               # no lick in the response window\n\nOUTPUT_VALUES = [\n    ['left', 'right', 'no lick'],\n    ...\n]\n```\n\n```python\n# choice is not stored; derive it from instruction x outcome\noutcome_s = trials['outcome'].values\nside = np.array([SIDE_CODE[x] for x in trials['trial_instruction'].values])\nchoice = np.where(outcome_s == 'ignore', CHOICE_NO_LICK,\n                  np.where(outcome_s == 'hit', side, 1 - side))\n\n# per-trial values are repeated across bins so all outputs share one array\nout = np.empty((n_trials, len(OUTPUT_NAMES), N_BINS), np.int8)\nout[:, 0, :] = choice[:, None]\n```\n\niii. `left = 0` and `right = 1` follow the instructions, and a third class is defined for the no-lick case. Choice is one value per trial, so it is repeated across the 80 bins to keep all four outputs in a single `(n_output, n_timepoints)` array.\n\n## 6-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *outcome* derived from?\n\ni. The trials table stores the outcome explicitly with exactly the three categories the instructions ask for, so no derivation is needed.\n\n## 6-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *outcome*?\n\ni. The three strings are mapped to `0` ignore, `1` miss, `2` hit via a fixed dictionary, and written into row 1 of the output array, repeated across all 80 bins.\n\nii.\n```python\nOUTCOME_CODE = {'ignore': 0, 'miss': 1, 'hit': 2}\n```\n\n```python\nout[:, 1, :] = np.array([OUTCOME_CODE[x] for x in outcome_s])[:, None]\n```\n\niii. The code assignment follows the instructions. Outcome is one value per trial, so it is repeated across bins like the other per-trial outputs.\n\n## 7-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *early_lick* derived from?\n\ni. From the `early_lick` column of the trials table, which holds the strings `'no early'` and `'early'`.\n\nii.\n```python\ntrials['early_lick'].values\n```\n\niii. The trials table flags early licking explicitly, so no derivation is needed. The lick that sets the flag occurs during the sample or delay epoch, before the go cue, so the event itself falls inside the -2.5 s window even though the flag is stored per trial.\n\n## 7-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *early_lick*?\n\ni. The two strings are mapped to `0` no, `1` yes via a fixed dictionary, and written into row 2 of the output array, repeated across all 80 bins.\n\nii.\n```python\nEARLY_CODE = {'no early': 0, 'early': 1}\n```\n\n```python\nout[:, 2, :] = np.array([EARLY_CODE[x] for x in trials['early_lick'].values])[:, None]\n```\n\niii. The code assignment follows the instructions. One value per trial, so repeated across bins like the other per-trial outputs.\n\n## 8-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *tongue_y_position* derived from?\n\ni. From `acquisition/BehavioralTimeSeries/Camera0_side_TongueTracking`, whose `data` is `(n_frames, 3)` = `tongue_x`, `tongue_y`, `tongue_likelihood`, with matching `timestamps`. Column 1 (`tongue_y`) is the value; column 2 (`likelihood`) decides whether the tongue is visible in that frame.\n\nii.\n```python\nts = nwb.acquisition['BehavioralTimeSeries'].time_series['Camera0_side_TongueTracking']\nt_cam = np.asarray(ts.timestamps)\ndata = ts.data[:]                        # (n_frames, 3) = x, y, likelihood\ny = data[:, 1].copy()\n```\n\niii. This is the only tongue measurement in the file. The channel layout is stated in the series' own `description` attribute (`\"('tongue_x', 'tongue_y', 'tongue_likelihood')\"`) rather than assumed. Tracking is present in all 174 sessions and runs at ~294 Hz.\n\n## 8-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *tongue_y_position*?\n\ni. Four steps. Frames with `likelihood < 0.5` are set to NaN, since the tracker still reports a position when the tongue is not protruding. The surviving values are averaged into 50 ms bins across the whole session, and the 40th and 60th percentiles of *those bin means* give two class edges. Each trial's window is then binned the same way and digitised against those edges, giving classes 0/1/2. Bins containing no visible frame are assigned a fourth class, `3` (`'not visible'`).\n\nii.\n```python\nTONGUE_CONF = 0.5                # tracking likelihood for the tongue to count as visible\nTONGUE_PCT = (40, 60)            # per-session percentiles -> 3 visible classes\nTONGUE_HIDDEN = 3                # no visible tongue frame in the bin\n```\n\n```python\ny[data[:, 2] < TONGUE_CONF] = np.nan     # keep only visible frames\n\n# bin the whole session on one grid, then take percentiles of the bin means\ngidx = np.floor((t_cam - t_cam[0]) / BIN).astype(np.int64)\nedges = np.nanpercentile(_bin_mean(gidx, y, int(gidx[-1]) + 1), TONGUE_PCT)\n\n# per trial, bin the same way and digitise against those edges\ncls = np.full((len(go), N_BINS), TONGUE_HIDDEN, np.int8)\nlo = np.searchsorted(t_cam, go + T_START, 'left')\nhi = np.searchsorted(t_cam, go + T_STOP, 'left')\nfor i in range(len(go)):\n    b = np.floor((t_cam[lo[i]:hi[i]] - (go[i] + T_START)) / BIN).astype(np.int64)\n    np.clip(b, 0, N_BINS - 1, out=b)\n    m = _bin_mean(b, y[lo[i]:hi[i]], N_BINS)\n    ok = ~np.isnan(m)\n    cls[i, ok] = np.digitize(m[ok], edges)\n```\n\niii. The tongue is visible in only ~10% of frames, and when it is retracted the tracker still emits a position, so those frames are discarded rather than averaged in — including them shifts the percentiles and mixes real protrusions with noise. The likelihood value is effectively binary (89% of frames below 0.01, 10.5% at or above 0.99), so the exact threshold does not matter.\n\nPercentiles are taken over the 50 ms bin means rather than raw frames so the edges are defined on the same quantity that gets discretised; taking them over frames instead skews the resulting class balance, because averaging within a bin pulls values toward the center. The 40th/60th split and the per-session scope both follow the instructions. A fourth class is defined for bins with no visible tongue, which is 75% of all bins.\n\n## 8-d. How is `output` *tongue_y_position* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. This is the one genuinely time-varying output, so it is the only one needing real alignment. The camera timestamps are on the same session-absolute clock as the spikes and the go cues, so each trial's frame range is found by `searchsorted` on the camera timestamps at `go + T_START` and `go + T_STOP`, and frames are assigned to bins by their offset from `go + T_START` — the same go-cue-relative grid used for the firing rates.\n\nii.\n```python\nlo = np.searchsorted(t_cam, go + T_START, 'left')\nhi = np.searchsorted(t_cam, go + T_STOP, 'left')\nfor i in range(len(go)):\n    b = np.floor((t_cam[lo[i]:hi[i]] - (go[i] + T_START)) / BIN).astype(np.int64)\n```\n\niii. The camera timestamps share the global clock with the spikes and events, so no interpolation or offset correction is needed — the same bin grid is applied to both streams, which guarantees bin `k` of the tongue output covers the same interval as bin `k` of the firing rates.\n\nUnlike the spikes, the video is trial-gated: it runs from `trials.start_time` to the trial-end event and is off during the inter-trial interval. On the ~3% of trials where the go cue falls less than 2.5 s after trial start, the leading bins therefore contain no frames at all and fall into the `'not visible'` class.\n\n## 9. How are minor mistakes in the data, e.g. missing data, handled?\n\ni. Three cases:\n\n- **Session never quality-controlled**: `classification` and `anno_name` are NaN rather than strings. `_text` maps any non-string entry to `''`, the session then has no `'good'` units, and it is dropped.\n- **Trials without spike data**: excluded via `obs_intervals` and `free_water` (see 1-e).\n- **Frames with no tracked tongue**: set to NaN and excluded from the bin mean; a bin left with no valid frame becomes the `'not visible'` class.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef _text(col):\n    \"\"\"Read a text column; non-str entries (unlabelled sessions) become ''.\"\"\"\n    return np.array([x if isinstance(x, str) else '' for x in col[:]])\n```\n\n```python\ngood = np.flatnonzero(cls == 'good')\nif good.size == 0:\n    return None\n```\n\n```python\ny[data[:, 2] < TONGUE_CONF] = np.nan     # keep only visible frames\n...\nm = _bin_mean(b, y[lo[i]:hi[i]], N_BINS)\nok = ~np.isnan(m)\ncls[i, ok] = np.digitize(m[ok], edges)\n```\n\niii. Missing data is handled one of two ways depending on what it means. Where nothing was recorded, the session or trial is excluded, since emitting it would fabricate data — a trial with no spikes would otherwise appear as 4 s of 0 Hz across every unit. Where the measurement legitimately has no value, as with a retracted tongue, it is represented as an explicit category rather than imputed.\n\n## 10-a. What are the most time-consuming steps of the code?\n\ni. Reading each NWB file dominates. The full conversion takes 247 s for 174 sessions, ~1.4 s per session on average, ranging from ~0.7 s to ~4 s roughly with unit count. Within a session the costs are pulling the `spike_times` buffer (up to ~11.5 M doubles) and the tongue tracking array (~680 k x 3), then the per-unit `searchsorted` loop. Pickling the 11.9 GB result takes a further ~40 s.\n\nii. N/A\n\niii. The work is dominated by I/O and by one binary search per bin edge per unit, both of which scale with the data actually needed. No step was worth optimising further given the 15-minute budget in the instructions.\n\n## 10-b. What loops in the code could have been vectorized to improve efficiency?\n\ni. Two loops remain. The per-unit loop in the neural binning runs one `searchsorted` per unit, but over *all* trials at once — the per-trial dimension is already vectorised by flattening the edge array. The per-trial loop in `tongue_output` bins one trial's frames at a time.\n\nii.\n```python\nedges = (go[:, None] + REL_EDGES[None, :]).ravel()\nfor r, u in enumerate(good):\n    s = allst[starts[u]:offs[u]]\n    pos = np.searchsorted(s, edges).reshape(n_trials, N_BINS + 1)\n```\n\niii. The per-unit loop cannot be collapsed further because each unit has a different number of spikes, so there is no single sorted array to search — this is inherent to the ragged storage. The tongue loop could be vectorised with a global bin index and one `bincount`, but it runs over trials rather than frames and is not a measurable share of runtime, so it was left in the clearer form.\n\n## 10-c. What processing does the code repeat multiple times?\n\ni. Nothing is recomputed. Each NWB file is opened once and every quantity derived from it is computed once. The bin grid (`REL_EDGES`, `CENTERS`) is built once at module level and reused for every trial and session.\n\nii. N/A\n\niii. The conversion is a single pass over the files. Because the tongue discretisation edges are per-session rather than global, they can be computed inside that same pass — no second pass over the data is needed to establish them.\n\n## 10-d. What unnecessary processing does the code do that is discarded in downstream analyses?\n\ni. None. Every field computed goes into the output.\n\nii. N/A\n\niii. N/A\n\n## 10-e. How is memory usage optimized?\n\ni. Firing rates are stored as `float32` and the outputs as `int8` rather than the default `float64`. Each session is read inside a `with` block so the file handle and its cached arrays are released on exit, and only the per-trial slices are retained. The full neural payload is 11.9 GB; a single session's working arrays are at most ~0.2 GB.\n\nii.\n```python\nrates = np.empty((good.size, n_trials, N_BINS), np.float32)\n```\n\n```python\nout = np.empty((n_trials, len(OUTPUT_NAMES), N_BINS), np.int8)\n```\n\n```python\ninp = np.stack([time_from_tone, photostim], axis=1).astype(np.float32)\n```\n\niii. `float32` halves the largest object in the output relative to `float64` at no cost, since firing rates are multiples of 20 Hz and well within its precision. Outputs are small non-negative integers, so `int8` is sufficient. Peak memory is set by the accumulated result rather than by any single session, so no further streaming was needed.","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false}}

NaN where a bin is empty.\"\"\"\n    ok = ~np.isnan(y)\n    tot = np.bincount(idx[ok], weights=y[ok], minlength=n)\n    cnt = np.bincount(idx[ok], minlength=n)\n    with np.errstate(invalid='ignore'):\n        return np.where(cnt > 0, tot / np.maximum(cnt, 1), np.nan)\n\n\ndef tongue_output(nwb, go):\n    \"\"\"Discretise tongue y into (n_trials, N_BINS) classes.\"\"\"\n    ts = nwb.acquisition['BehavioralTimeSeries'].time_series['Camera0_side_TongueTracking']\n    t_cam = np.asarray(ts.timestamps)\n    data = ts.data[:]                        # (n_frames, 3) = x, y, likelihood\n    y = data[:, 1].copy()\n    y[data[:, 2] < TONGUE_CONF] = np.nan     # keep only visible frames\n\n    # bin the whole session on one grid, then take percentiles of the bin means\n    gidx = np.floor((t_cam - t_cam[0]) / BIN).astype(np.int64)\n    edges = np.nanpercentile(_bin_mean(gidx, y, int(gidx[-1]) + 1), TONGUE_PCT)\n\n    # per trial, bin the same way and digitise against those edges\n    cls = np.full((len(go), N_BINS), TONGUE_HIDDEN, np.int8)\n    lo = np.searchsorted(t_cam, go + T_START, 'left')\n    hi = np.searchsorted(t_cam, go + T_STOP, 'left')\n    for i in range(len(go)):\n        b = np.floor((t_cam[lo[i]:hi[i]] - (go[i] + T_START)) / BIN).astype(np.int64)\n        np.clip(b, 0, N_BINS - 1, out=b)\n        m = _bin_mean(b, y[lo[i]:hi[i]], N_BINS)\n        ok = ~np.isnan(m)\n        cls[i, ok] = np.digitize(m[ok], edges)\n    return cls\n\n\ndef process_session(path):\n    \"\"\"Convert one NWB file. Returns a dict, or None if the session is dropped.\"\"\"\n    with NWBHDF5IO(path, mode='r', load_namespaces=True) as io:\n        nwb = io.read()\n        units = nwb.units\n        trials = nwb.trials.to_dataframe()\n        bev = nwb.acquisition['BehavioralEvents'].time_series\n\n        # ---- units: QC classifier verdict ----------------------------------\n        cls = _text(units['classification'])\n        good = np.flatnonzero(cls == 'good')\n        if good.size == 0:\n            return None\n        regions = [region_label(a) for a in _text(units['anno_name'])[good]]\n\n        # all NWB times are session-absolute seconds\n        go = np.asarray(bev['go_start_times'].timestamps)\n        assert len(go) == len(trials), f'{len(go)} go cues vs {len(trials)} trials'\n\n        # ---- trials filtering ----\n        # spike data\n        # obs_intervals gives one [start, stop] per observed trial, which in some\n        # sessions is fewer than the behavioural trials. \n        # a behavioural trial is observed if its start time is one of the interval starts\n        obs = np.asarray(units['obs_intervals'][good[0]])        \n        keep = np.isin(trials['start_time'].values, obs[:, 0])\n        assert keep.sum() == len(obs), f'{keep.sum()} matched vs {len(obs)} observed'\n\n        # filter no water trials\n        keep &= trials['free_water'].values == 0\n        \n        # a sessioon has to have at least 2 trials according to the instruction\n        if keep.sum() < 2:\n            return None\n        \n        trials = trials[keep]\n        go = go[keep]\n        n_trials = len(go)\n\n        # last tone onset before the go cue; an early lick replays the sample epoch\n        sample = np.asarray(bev['sample_start_times'].timestamps)\n        tone = sample[np.searchsorted(sample, go, side='left') - 1]\n\n        # ---- inputs --------------------------------------------------------\n        # bins sit around the go cue, values are seconds since the tone\n        time_from_tone = CENTERS[None, :] + (go - tone)[:, None]\n\n        # photostim onsets are stored as strings relative to trial start\n        on_s = trials['photostim_onset'].values\n        has = on_s != 'N/A'\n        stim_on = np.full(n_trials, np.nan)\n        stim_on[has] = trials['start_time'].values[has] + on_s[has].astype(float) - go[has]\n        stim_off = np.full(n_trials, np.nan)\n        stim_off[has] = stim_on[has] + trials['photostim_duration'].values[has].astype(float)\n        # NaN compares False, so non-stim trials stay 0\n        photostim = ((CENTERS[None, :] >= stim_on[:, None])\n                     & (CENTERS[None, :] < stim_off[:, None]))\n\n        inp = np.stack([time_from_tone, photostim], axis=1).astype(np.float32)\n\n        # ---- outputs -------------------------------------------------------\n        # choice is not stored; derive it from instruction x outcome\n        outcome_s = trials['outcome'].values\n        side = np.array([SIDE_CODE[x] for x in trials['trial_instruction'].values])\n        choice = np.where(outcome_s == 'ignore', CHOICE_NO_LICK,\n                          np.where(outcome_s == 'hit', side, 1 - side))\n\n        # per-trial values are repeated across bins so all outputs share one array\n        out = np.empty((n_trials, len(OUTPUT_NAMES), N_BINS), np.int8)\n        out[:, 0, :] = choice[:, None]\n        out[:, 1, :] = np.array([OUTCOME_CODE[x] for x in outcome_s])[:, None]\n        out[:, 2, :] = np.array([EARLY_CODE[x] for x in trials['early_lick'].values])[:, None]\n        out[:, 3, :] = tongue_output(nwb, go)\n\n        # ---- neural --------------------------------------------------------\n        # spike_times is ragged: read the buffer once instead of one read per unit\n        offs = np.asarray(units['spike_times'].data)\n        allst = np.asarray(units['spike_times'].target.data)\n        starts = np.concatenate([[0], offs[:-1]])\n\n        edges = (go[:, None] + REL_EDGES[None, :]).ravel()\n        rates = np.empty((good.size, n_trials, N_BINS), np.float32)\n        for r, u in enumerate(good):\n            s = allst[starts[u]:offs[u]]\n            # running spike total at each edge; differencing gives the count per bin\n            pos = np.searchsorted(s, edges).reshape(n_trials, N_BINS + 1)\n            rates[r] = np.diff(pos, axis=1)\n        rates /= BIN                                  # counts -> Hz\n\n        return {\n            'session_id': nwb.identifier,\n            'subject': nwb.subject.subject_id,\n            'neural': [np.ascontiguousarray(rates[:, t, :]) for t in range(n_trials)],\n            'input': [inp[t] for t in range(n_trials)],\n            'output': [out[t] for t in range(n_trials)],\n            'regions': regions,\n            'n_trials': n_trials,\n            'n_units': int(good.size),\n        }\n\n\ndef assemble(results):\n    \"\"\"Stitch per-session results into the target dictionary.\"\"\"\n    subjects = sorted({r['subject'] for r in results})\n    sub_ix = {s: i for i, s in enumerate(subjects)}\n\n    brain_regions = sorted({g for r in results for g in r['regions']})\n    reg_ix = {g: i for i, g in enumerate(brain_regions)}\n\n    return {\n        'neural': [r['neural'] for r in results],\n        'input': [r['input'] for r in results],\n        'output': [r['output'] for r in results],\n\n        'subjects': subjects,\n        'subject_idx': np.array([sub_ix[r['subject']] for r in results], np.int32),\n\n        'brain_regions': brain_regions,\n        'brain_region_idx': [np.array([reg_ix[g] for g in r['regions']], np.int32)\n                             for r in results],\n\n        'input_names': INPUT_NAMES,\n        'output_names': OUTPUT_NAMES,\n        'output_values': OUTPUT_VALUES,\n\n        'metadata': {\n            'task_description':\n                'Auditory delayed-response task. A 3 kHz or 12 kHz tone (0.65 s '\n                'sample epoch) instructs a left or right lick, followed by a 1.2 s '\n                'delay; a 6 kHz go cue opens a 1.5 s response window. Licking '\n                'during sample or delay replays that epoch. 97\t\n    98\t    The reference concatenates classifier-good units across probes even when their\n    99\t    manually accepted observation periods differ, so requiring every unit would\n   100\t    discard valid neural population data. We reject only periods outside all kept\n   101\t    units, which otherwise become entirely zero trials.\n   102\t    \"\"\"\n   103\t    valid = np.zeros(len(starts), dtype=bool)\n   104\t    for unit_idx in good_indices:\n   105\t        intervals = np.asarray(units[\"obs_intervals\"][int(unit_idx)], dtype=np.float64)\n   106\t        intervals = np.atleast_2d(intervals)\n   107\t        covered = np.zeros(len(starts), dtype=bool)\n   108\t        for onset, offset in intervals:\n   109\t            # The final alignment check below uses go-based extraction boundaries;\n   110\t            # this first pass cheaply rejects trials wholly outside recording time.\n   111\t            covered |= (starts >= onset) & (stops <= offset)\n   112\t        valid |= covered\n   113\t    return valid\n   114\t\n   115\t\n   116\tdef _bin_spikes(\n   117\t    units,\n   118\t    good_indices: np.ndarray,\n   119\t    go_times: np.ndarray,\n   120\t) -> np.ndarray:\n   121\t    \"\"\"Vectorized per-unit spike binning; result is trials x neurons x time.\"\"\"\n   122\t    absolute_edges = go_times[:, None] + BIN_EDGES[None, :]\n   123\t    # Flattened edges are sorted for this task (successive go cues are >4 s apart),\n   124\t    # allowing one searchsorted call per neuron rather than one per trial.\n   125\t    flattened = absolute_edges.ravel()\n   126\t    monotonic = np.all(flattened[1:] >= flattened[:-1])\n   127\t    rates = np.empty((len(go_times), len(good_indices), N_TIME), dtype=np.float32)\n   128\t    for out_idx, unit_idx in enumerate(good_indices):\n   129\t        spikes = _unit_spike_times(units, int(unit_idx))\n   130\t        if monotonic:\n   131\t            positions = np.searchsorted(spikes, flattened, side=\"left\")\n   132\t            counts = np.diff(positions.reshape(len(go_times), N_TIME + 1), axis=1)\n   133\t        else:\n   134\t            counts = np.stack([\n   135\t                np.diff(np.searchsorted(spikes, row, side=\"left\"))\n   136\t                for row in absolute_edges\n   137\t            ])\n   138\t        rates[:, out_idx, :] = counts.astype(np.float32) / BIN_WIDTH\n   139\t    return rates\n   140\t\n   141\t\n   142\tdef _safe_session_name(path: Path) -> str:\n   143\t    return re.sub(r\"[^A-Za-z0-9_.-]+\", \"_\", path.stem)\n   144\t\n   145\t\n   146\tdef _plot_processing(\n   147\t    path: Path,\n   148\t    go_times: np.ndarray,\n   149\t    tone_times: np.ndarray,\n   150\t    inputs: np.ndarray,\n   151\t    outputs: np.ndarray,\n   152\t    rates: np.ndarray,\n   153\t    tongue_times: np.ndarray,\n   154\t    tongue_y: np.ndarray,\n   155\t    tongue_likelihood: np.ndarray,\n   156\t    tongue_thresholds: tuple[float, float],\n   157\t) -> None:\n   158\t    \"\"\"Plot the alignment, binning, inputs, and tongue discretization.\"\"\"\n   159\t    trial = min(2, len(go_times) - 1)\n   160\t    rel_video = tongue_times - go_times[trial]\n   161\t    video_mask = (rel_video >= OFF_START) & (rel_video <= OFF_END)\n   162\t    mean_rate = rates[trial].mean(axis=0)\n   163\t\n   164\t    fig, axes = plt.subplots(3, 2, figsize=(15, 12), sharex=False)\n   165\t    ax = axes.ravel()\n   166\t    ax[0].imshow(\n   167\t        rates[trial, : min(80, rates.shape[1])], aspect=\"auto\", origin=\"lower\",\n   168\t        extent=[OFF_START, OFF_END, 0, min(80, rates.shape[1])], cmap=\"magma\"\n   169\t    )\n   170\t    ax[0].axvline(0, color=\"cyan\", lw=1)\n   171\t    ax[0].set(title=\"50-ms go-aligned firing rates\", ylabel=\"good unit\")\n   172\t\n   173\t    ax[1].plot(BIN_CENTERS, mean_rate, color=\"black\")\n   174\t    ax[1].axvline(0, color=\"tab:red\", label=\"go\")\n   175\t    ax[1].axvline(tone_times[trial] - go_times[trial], color=\"tab:blue\", label=\"tone\")\n   176\t    ax[1].set(title=\"Population mean and event alignment\", ylabel=\"rate (Hz)\")\n   177\t    ax[1].legend()\n   178\t\n   179\t    ax[2].plot(BIN_CENTERS, inputs[trial, 0], label=\"time since tone\")\n   180\t    ax[2].step(BIN_CENTERS, inputs[trial, 1], where=\"mid\", label=\"laser on\")\n   181\t    ax[2].set(title=\"Decoder inputs\", ylabel=\"value\")\n   182\t    ax[2].legend()\n   183\t\n   184\t    ax[3].plot(rel_video[video_mask], tongue_y[video_mask], lw=.5, color=\"0.5\")\n   185\t    visible = video_mask & (tongue_likelihood >= DLC_VISIBLE_THRESHOLD)\n   186\t    ax[3].scatter(rel_video[visible], tongue_y[visible], s=2, color=\"tab:blue\")\n   187\t    for threshold in tongue_thresholds:\n   188\t        ax[3].axhline(threshold, color=\"tab:orange\", ls=\"--\")\n   189\t    ax[3].set(title=\"Raw tongue y, visible frames, 40/60% thresholds\", ylabel=\"pixels\")\n   190\t\n   191\t    ax[4].plot(rel_video[video_mask], tongue_likelihood[video_mask], lw=.5)\n   192\t    ax[4].axhline(DLC_VISIBLE_THRESHOLD, color=\"tab:red\", ls=\"--\")\n   193\t    ax[4].set(title=\"DLC tongue visibility\", ylabel=\"likelihood\", ylim=(-.05, 1.05))\n   194\t\n   195\t    ax[5].step(BIN_CENTERS, outputs[trial, 3], where=\"mid\", label=\"tongue class\")\n   196\t    ax[5].set(\n   197\t        title=(f\"Output classes; choice={outputs[trial,0,0]}, \"\n   198\t               f\"outcome={outputs[trial,1,0]}, early={outputs[trial,2,0]}\"),\n   199\t        ylabel=\"class\", yticks=[0, 1, 2, 3]\n   200\t    )\n   201\t    for a in ax:\n   202\t        a.set_xlabel(\"time from go cue (s)\")\n   203\t        a.grid(alpha=.15)\n   204\t    fig.suptitle(path.name)\n   205\t    fig.tight_layout()\n   206\t    fig.savefig(Path(\"/app\") / f\"processing_{_safe_session_name(path)}.png\", dpi=150)\n   207\t    plt.close(fig)\n   208\t\n   209\t\n   210\tdef convert_session(path: Path, show_processing: bool = False) -> dict | None:\n   211\t    \"\"\"Convert one NWB file, returning None when it has no classifier-good units.\"\"\"\n   212\t    started = time.perf_counter()\n   213\t    with NWBHDF5IO(str(path), \"r\", load_namespaces=True) as io:\n   214\t        nwb = io.read()\n   215\t        units = nwb.units\n   216\t        classifications = np.asarray(units[\"classification\"][:]).astype(str)\n   217\t        good_indices = np.flatnonzero(classifications == \"good\")\n   218\t        if len(good_indices) == 0:\n   219\t            print(f\"SKIP {path.name}: no classifier-good units\", flush=True)\n   220\t            return None\n   221\t        annotations_all = np.asarray(units[\"anno_name\"][:]).astype(str)\n   222\t        annotations = annotations_all[good_indices]\n   223\t        if np.any(annotations == \"\"):\n   224\t            raise ValueError(f\"{path.name}: classifier-good unit lacks CCF annotation\")\n   225\t\n   226\t        trials_all = nwb.trials.to_dataframe()\n   227\t        starts_all = trials_all[\"start_time\"].to_numpy(np.float64)\n   228\t        stops_all = trials_all[\"stop_time\"].to_numpy(np.float64)\n   229\t        trials = trials_all.copy()\n   230\t        starts = trials[\"start_time\"].to_numpy(np.float64)\n   231\t        stops = trials[\"stop_time\"].to_numpy(np.float64)\n   232\t        if len(trials) < 2:\n   233\t            print(f\"SKIP {path.name}: fewer than two commonly observed trials\", flush=True)\n   234\t            return None\n   235\t        go_by_trial = _events_by_trial(_events(nwb, \"go_start_times\"), starts, stops)\n   236\t        sample_by_trial = _events_by_trial(_events(nwb, \"sample_start_times\"), starts, stops)\n   237\t        if not all(len(x) == 1 for x in go_by_trial):\n   238\t            bad = [i for i, x in enumerate(go_by_trial) if len(x) != 1]\n   239\t            raise ValueError(f\"{path.name}: trials without exactly one go event: {bad[:10]}\")\n   240\t        if not all(len(x) >= 1 for x in sample_by_trial):\n   241\t            bad = [i for i, x in go_times)])\n   246\t\n   247\t        stim_starts_by_trial = _events_by_trial(\n   248\t            _events(nwb, \"photostim_start_times\"), starts, stops\n   249\t        )\n   250\t enumerate(zip(stim_starts_by_trial, stim_stops_by_trial)):\n   254\t            if len(on) != len(off):\n   255\t                raise ValueError(f\"{path.name}: unpaired laser events in trial {i}\")\n   256\t\n   257\t        absolute_centers = go_times[:, None] + BIN_CENTERS[None, :]\n   258\t        inputs = np.empty((len(trials), 2, N_TIME), dtype=np.float32)\n   259\t        inputs[:, stim_stops_by_trial)):\n   262\t            for onset, offset in zip(onsets, offsets):\n   263\t                active = ((absolute_centers[i] >= onset) &\n   264\t                          (absolute_centers[i] < offset))\n   265\t                inputs[i, 1, active] = 1.0\n   266\t\n   267\t        behavior = nwb.acquisition[\"BehavioralTimeSeries\"]\n   268\t        tongue = behavior.time_series[\"Camera0_side_TongueTracking\"]\n   269\t        tongue_times = np.asarray(tongue.timestamps[:], dtype=np.float64)\n   270\t        tongue_data = np.asarray(tongue.data[:], np.isfinite(tongue_likelihood) & (\n   274\t            tongue_likelihood >= DLC_VISIBLE_THRESHOLD\n   275\t        )\n   276\t        if session_visible.sum() < 2:\n   277\t            raise ValueError(f\"{path.name}: insufficient visible tongue samples\")\n   278\t        q40, q60 = np.quantile(tongue_y[session_visible], [0.4, 0.6])\n   279\t        nearest = _nearest_indices(tongue_times, absolute_centers.ravel()).reshape(\n   280\t            len(trials), N_TIME\n   281\t        )\n   282\t        matched_y = tongue_y[nearest]\n   283\t        matched_likelihood = tongue_likelihood[nearest]\n   284\t        visible = np.isfinite(matched_y) & np.isfinite(matched_likelihood) & (\n   285\t            matched_likelihood >= DLC_VISIBLE_THRESHOLD\n   286\t        )\n   287\t        tongue_class = np.full((len(trials), N_TIME), 3, dtype=np.int8)\n   288\t        tongue_class[visible & (matched_y < q40)] = 0\n   289\t        tongue_class[visible & (matched_y >= q40) & (matched_y <= q60)] = 1\n   290\t        tongue_class[visible & (matched_y > q60)] = 2\n   291\t\n   292\t        outcome_map = {\"ignore\": 0, \"miss\": 1, \"hit\": 2}\n   293\t        early_map = {\"no early\": 0, \"early\": 1}\n   294\t        outcomes_text = trials[\"outcome\"].astype(str).to_numpy()\n   295\t        instructions = _classify_choice(inst, outcome)\n   299\t            for inst, outcome in zip(instructions, outcomes_text)\n   300\t        ], dtype=np.int8)\n   301\t        outcome = np.asarray([outcome_map[x] for dtype=np.int8)\n   304\t        outputs[:, 0, :] = choice[:, None]\n   305\t        outputs[:, 1, :] = outcome[:, None]\n   306\t        outputs[:, 2, :] = early[:, None]\n   307\t        outputs[:, 3, :] = tongue_class\n   308\t\n   309\t        rates = _bin_spikes(units, good_indices, go_times)\n   310\t        if not np.isfinite(rates).all() or np.any(rates < 0):\n   311\t            raise ValueError(f\"{path.name}: invalid firing rates\")\n   312\t        if not np.allclose(rates / (1 / BIN_WIDTH), np.round(rates / (1 / BIN_WIDTH))):\n   313\t            raise ValueError(f\"{path.name}: rates are not integer-count multiples\")\n   314\t\n   315\t        # NWBs can include behavioral trials after every ephys probe stopped. The\n   316\t        # reference bins all classifier-good units without applying per-unit manual\n   317\t        # observation masks; preserve that logic, but discard windows with no spike\n   318\t        # from any retained unit because they contain no neural recording at all.\n   319\t        neural_present = np.any(rates != 0, axis=(1, 2))\n   320\t        n_excluded_no_neural = int((~neural_present).sum())\n   321\t        if n_excluded_no_neural:\n   322\t            trials = trials.loc[neural_present].copy()\n   323\t            go_times = go_times[neural_present]\n   324\t            tone_times = tone_times[neural_present]\n   325\t            inputs = inputs[neural_present]\n   326\t            outputs = outputs[neural_present]\n   327\t            rates = 331\t\n   332\t        if show_processing:\n   333\t            _plot_processing(\n   334\t                path, go_times, tone_times, inputs, outputs, rates, tongue_times,\n   335\t                tongue_y, tongue_likelihood, (float(q40), float(q60))\n   336\t            )\n   337\t\n   338\t        result = {\n   339\t            \"path\": str(path),\n   340\t            \"session_id\": 350\t                \"n_neurons\": len(good_indices),\n   351\t                \"tone_to_go_s\": [float(np.min(go_times - tone_times)),\n   352\t \"tongue_visible_fraction_at_bins\": float(visible.mean()),\n   356\t                \"photostim_trial_count\": int(sum(len(x) > 0 for x in stim_starts_by_trial)),\n   357\t s\", flush=True\n   369\t    )\n   370\t    return result\n   371\t\n   372\t\n   373\tdef validate_converted(data: dict) -> None:\n   374\t    \"\"\"Fast structural and semantic checks before writing the pickle.\"\"\"\n   375\t    ns = len(data[\"neural\"])\n   376\t    assert ns == len(data[\"input\"]) == len(data[\"output\"])\n   377\t    assert ns == len(data[\"subject_idx\"]) == len(data[\"brain_region_idx\"])\n   378\t    assert len(data[\"metadata\"][\"session_info\"]) == ns\n   379\t    for s in range(ns):\n   380\t        nt = len(data[\"neural\"][s])\n   381\t == 0) | (inp[1] == 1))\n   390\t            assert np.all(np.diff(inp[0]) > 0)\n   391\t            assert np.all((out[0] >= 0) & (out[0] <= 2))\n   392\t            assert np.all((out[1] >= 0) &          \"bin_centers_relative_to_go_s\": BIN_CENTERS.tolist(),\n   441\t            \"neural_measure\": \"firing rate (Hz), spike counts / 0.05 s\",\n   442\t            \"bin_interval_convention\":   71\t    if outcome == \"ignore\":\n    72\t        return 2\n    73\t    if outcome == \"hit\":\n    74\t        return 0 if instruction == \"left\" else 1\n    75\t    if outcome == \"miss\":\n 97\t\n    98\t    The reference concatenates classifier-good units across probes even when their\n    99\t    manually accepted observation periods differ, so requiring every unit would\n   100\t \"\"\"Vectorized per-unit spike binning; result is trials x neurons x time.\"\"\"\n   122\t    absolute_edges = go_times[:, None] + BIN_EDGES[None, :]\n   123\t    # Flattened edges are sorted for combines probes; aligns lick/stimulation times to go cue. |\n| `helper_get_neuron_id_area` | `code/VideoAnalysisUtils/preprocessing_DJ_2022Aug.py` | CURATION | Intersects classifier-selected good-unit IDs with hemisphere and valid histology/CCF annotation. |\n| `sliding_histogram` | `code/VideoAnalysisUtils/preprocessing_DJ_2022Aug.py` | PROCESSING | Counts spikes in half-open sliding windows and divides by bin width to obtain Hz. |\n| `process_one_area` | `code/VideoAnalysisUtils/preprocessing_DJ_2022Aug.py` | PROCESSING | Truncates go-cue-relative spikes, bins firing rates, and stores per-area neural/task metadata. |\n| `load_session` | `code/VideoAnalysisUtils/population_decoding_utils.py` | LOADING | Concatenates processed area files across neurons into one session tensor and CCF arrays. |\n| `get_regular_trial_mask` | `code/VideoAnalysisUtils/population_decoding_utils.py` and `functions_for_r2.py` | CURATION | Defines regular trials: no early lick, auto/free water, no-response, or stimulation. |\n| `create_4fold_trial_type_mask` | `code/VideoAnalysisUtils/functions_for_r2.py` | PROCESSING | Stratifies trials as hit/miss crossed with left/right; pools sparse miss groups. |\n\n### Notes\n- The repository README identifies the source as DANDI 000363 and the paper as *Brain-wide analysis reveals movement encoding structured across and within brain areas*.\n- This is extracellular electrophysiology, not imaging; delta-F/F is not applicable.\n- Reference preprocessing uses classifier QC (`qc_mode='classifier'`). Good units are supplied by session-level QC files, restricted to units with matched ephys/histology and one of 14 broad regions, and split at CCF ML coordinate 5700 into hemispheres.\n- Raw spike times are already relative to go cue. Lick and laser times originally referenced to trial start are shifted by subtracting the go-cue timestamp.\n- The reusable binning definition is `[center-width/2, center+width/2)` and firing rate is spike count / width. The method-paper production run used 40-ms windows with 3.4-ms stride over -3 to +3 s (`Sherlock/preprocess_all_ephys.py`); another script default uses 100-ms width/50-ms stride. The decoder requirement overrides these with non-overlapping 50-ms bins from -2.5 to +1.5 s.\n- Important source behavior fields: early report, auto/free water, lick directions/times, correctness (1 hit, 0 error, -1 no response), go cue, sample/delay durations, stimulation `[power,type,on,off]`, and instructed trial type.\n- Reference population analyses generally exclude early-lick, auto-water, free-water, no-response, and stimulation trials. For this requested decoder, early lick, no-lick outcome, and photostimulation are explicit targets/inputs, so those categories cannot be discarded merely by applying the regular-trial mask; this necessary divergence will be resolved after inspecting NWB availability.\n- `load_session` concatenates all areas within a session along neurons and preserves per-neuron CCF labels, matching the target session organization.\n\n---\n\n## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Data Structure\n- `/app/data/dandiset.yaml` identifies DANDI 000363 version 0.230822.0128, 53,585,631,708 bytes (~50 GiB), mouse electrophysiology/behavior, 174 NWB assets, and 28 subjects.\n- Files are organized as `sub-<subject>/sub-<subject>_ses-<timestamp>_behavior+ecephys[+ogen].nwb`. All native-data inspection was performed through `pynwb.NWBHDF5IO`; no HDF5 parser was used.\n- All 174 files have the same 14-column `trials` table: start/stop, trial number, photostim onset/power/duration, globally unique trial ID, task/protocol, left/right instruction, early-lick label, outcome, auto-water, and free-water. Task is uniformly `audio delay`.\n- `acquisition/BehavioralEvents` holds absolute timestamps for presample/sample/delay/go/trial-end boundaries, left/right licks, and (where present) photostimulation start/stop. Event-list counts can exceed trial counts for malformed/repeated state-machine events, so trial association must be based on trial intervals rather than positional indexing except for the one-go-per-trial stream after verification.\n- `acquisition/BehavioralTimeSeries` has side-camera Jaw, Nose, and Tongue tracking in every session; each is `(samples,3)` = x, y, likelihood with explicit timestamps. Some sessions add whisker/lick-port or Camera3 duplicates; Camera0 side tongue is consistent across all sessions.\n- `units` contains global session-clock spike times plus spike-sorting metrics, `classification`, detailed Allen CCF `anno_name`, per-unit valid-trial flags, observation intervals, electrode links, and waveforms. Raw units total 272,227. Classifier-good and annotated units normally coincide. One session (`sub-440958_ses-20190216T162508...`) has `classification='nan'` for all 1,852 units but all have nonempty annotations; thus 71,305 nonempty annotated units versus 69,453 literally labeled `good`. This anomaly requires reference-text/code reconciliation before final curation.\n- There are four acquisition-schema variants caused only by optional tracked features/cameras; required trial, neural, event, and Camera0 tongue streams are present in all sessions. All 174 files opened successfully.\n\n### Dataset Size (from data files)\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| Neurons (total) | 272,227 raw; 69,453 `classification=good`; 71,305 with nonempty CCF annotation |\n| Neurons / session | Raw: 493-3,191, mean 1,564.5, median 1,571.5; literal classifier-good: 0-923, mean 399.2, median 390 |\n| Subjects | 28 |\n| Sessions / subject | 3-10 (174 sessions total) |\n| Trials (total) | 94,990 |\n| Trials / session | 264-800, mean 545.9, median 534 |\n\nNative categorical totals: outcome = 65,254 hit / 15,641 miss / 14,095 ignore; early lick = 84,185 no / 10,805 yes; instruction = 46,077 left / 48,913 right. These are pre-curation counts.\n\n---\n\n## Step 3: Reference Text Reading\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Expected Statistics (from papers/methods)\n| Statistic | Value | Source Quote |\n|-----------|-------|--------------|\n| Neurons (total) | 69,943 good units (25.9% of Kilosort2 clusters) | Data paper Methods and QC white paper: “69,943 good units recorded across 173 behavioral sessions” |\n| Neurons / session | Not stated as a distribution | — |\n| Subjects | 28 mice | Both papers’ Methods |\n| Sessions / subject | 173 behavioral sessions total; 655 probe insertions (QC white paper; data-paper text elsewhere says 660 penetrations) | QC/Methods |\n| Trials (total) | Not stated | — |\n| Trials / session | Mean 476; range 130–785 | Data paper Methods |\n| Neural data time bin | Method paper: 40-ms width, 3.4-ms stride; data paper varies by analysis (40-ms/17-ms for video-to-neural, 200-ms/10-ms for population choice decoding) | Methods sections |\n| Behavior data time bin | Video acquired at 300 Hz (~3.33 ms/frame) | Both papers |\n| Reward rate | 84% correct mean, 65–99% session range on selected control/non-early trials | Data paper Methods |\n| Session eligibility | >65% overall performance and >=50 correct left and >=50 correct right trials | Data paper Methods |\n| Photostimulation prevalence | ~25% randomly interleaved trials in 17 VGAT-ChR2 mice | Data paper Methods |\n| Bilateral photostim performance | 83.2% control to 71.7% stimulated; 17 mice, 93 sessions | Data paper Methods |\n\n\n### Processing Details\n- Task: three 150-ms presentations of either 3-kHz or 12-kHz tone separated by 100-ms gaps; then 1.2-s delay; 100-ms go cue; 1.5-s answer period. Early licking replays the sample/delay epoch.\n- Spikes were Kilosort2 sorted. The method paper bins firing rates at 40-ms width/3.4-ms stride; requested downstream specification instead fixes 50-ms width and the -2.5-to-+1.5-s go-aligned interval.\n- Video/marker analysis uses side view, 300-Hz tracking. Reference marker cleaning detects velocity outliers at five SD and imputes from nearby frames. Occluded/in-mouth tongue positions were replaced by their mean for neural-prediction analyses. The requested decoder instead explicitly requires a `not visible` class, so visibility must be retained rather than mean-imputed.\n- Photoinhibition occurs in the last 0.5 s of delay and ends before go cue (including 100-ms ramp-down). This provides a strong timing sanity check for the binary photostimulation input.\n\n### Curation Steps\n\n**Neuron curation rules**:\nUse region-specific logistic-regression QC classifiers trained from manually curated Kilosort2 clusters using 15 quality metrics. “Good” classifier units were used in paper analyses. Cortex classifier covered cortex/hippocampus/olfactory/cortical subplate; striatum classifier covered striatum/pallidum; thalamus covered thalamus/hypothalamus; midbrain covered midbrain/pons; medulla covered medulla/cerebellum. QC ROC AUC averaged >0.9. The method paper additionally excluded <2-Hz neurons for its video-to-neural regression, but this is analysis-specific and not a global dataset curation rule.\n\n**Trial curation rules**:\nData-paper core analyses exclude early-lick and no-response trials and select high-performance sessions. Method-paper analyses exclude photoinhibition, free-water, early-lick, and ignore trials; behavioral prediction further uses only correct trials. These exclusions directly conflict with requested decoding of photostimulation, early lick, and ignore/no-lick outcomes, so retaining those trials is required unless a trial lacks the required alignment/data window. Auto/free-water trials require careful treatment because outcome/choice semantics may be non-volitional.\n\n### Decoders Trained\n| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n| Population lick choice | Logistic regression, 200-ms causal windows/10-ms step, nested 5-fold CV; the paper reports time-varying figure curves rather than a directly comparable single scalar. Chance = 0.5; strongest late-delay performance is in ALM. |\n| Behavioral choice from video | ROC AUC with 20-fold stratified CV on correct trials; figure curves, not a scalar directly comparable to this four-output decoder. |\n\nNo reference decoder predicts the requested three-way outcome, binary early lick, or four-way tongue-y class in the same formulation, so there is no paper accuracy directly comparable to those outputs.\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Discrepancies Found\n| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Papers say | Resolution |\n|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n| Session count | Preprocessing processes every raw session that has QC files | 174 NWBs; exactly one has no `good` classifier labels | 173 behavioral sessions | Exclude the zero-good-unit session naturally because it cannot yield a neural decoder session; remaining session count is exactly 173. |\n| Good-unit count | Classifier-QC IDs intersected with valid CCF/histology | 69,453 units labeled `classification='good'`; all have nonempty `anno_name` | 69,943 good units | Use the release’s explicit classifier labels, not reverse-engineered metric thresholds or the paper aggregate. Auto/free-water semantics will be retained only if requested labels remain defined; record flags in metadata and validate distributions. |\n| Tongue missingness | Method paper mean-imputes occluded tongue after five-SD velocity outlier cleanup | NWB exposes raw DLC y and likelihood | Requested class 3 = not visible | Preserve invisibility as class 3; do not mean-impute invisible values. Apply reference velocity-outlier concept only if needed, and use DLC confidence/finite position to establish visibility. |\n| Alignment | Raw reference export spikes already go-aligned; lick/laser shifted to go | NWB stores all streams in absolute session time | Both papers organize epochs relative to go | Use each NWB trial’s absolute go event, then subtract it consistently from spike, event, and video timestamps. Associate events by trial intervals to avoid state-machine replay count mismatches. |\n| Trial-count expectation | Code filters according to each downstream analysis | NWB raw mean 545.9, range 264–800 | Paper says mean 476, range 130–785 after experimental selection/definitions | Do not force counts to the publication aggregate: requested outputs require trials excluded by paper analyses, and the archived release differs. Report raw/retained counts and exclusion reasons explicitly. |\n\nFinal consistent understanding: each retained session is one NWB file; classifier-good, CCF-annotated units are concatenated within that session; global timestamps are aligned to the trial’s go onset; requested categories necessitate broader trial retention than either reference analysis; and the target’s 50-ms bins replace the papers’ analysis-specific sliding bins while retaining their counting convention.\n\n---\n\n## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Variable Mapping\n| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n| `units.spike_times` for `classification == 'good'` | `neural` | Count spikes in 80 half-open, non-overlapping 50-ms bins spanning edges [-2.5,+1.5] relative to go; divide by 0.05 s; float32 `(neurons,80)` | `sliding_histogram`, `process_one_area` | One session per NWB; global timestamps shifted by trial go timestamp. |\n| Last `sample_start_times` event before go in the same trial | `input[0]` | For each bin center, seconds since tone/sample onset: `(go + relative_bin_center) - tone_onset` | `process_one_sess` event alignment | Last onset handles early-lick state-machine replays and yields the final completed sample epoch; normal onset is ~1.85 s before go. |\n| `photostim_start_times` / `photostim_stop_times` | `input[1]` | 1 where absolute bin center is in `[laser_on, laser_off)`, else 0 | `process_one_sess` stimulation alignment | Pair events within each trial interval; expected during final 0.5 s before go and off by go. |\n| `trial_instruction` + `outcome` | `output[0]` choice | ignore -> no lick; hit -> instructed side; miss -> opposite side | Reference behavior fields in `process_one_sess` | More reliable task-defined response than isolated lick-event glitches; audited against response licks and agrees overwhelmingly. Values: left=0, right=1, no lick=2. |\n| `trials.outcome` | `output[1]` outcome | Direct categorical map ignore=0, miss=1, hit=2 | `correctness` mapping in reference loader | Retain ignore trials because explicitly requested. |\n| `trials.early_lick` | `output[2]` early lick | `no early`=0, `early`=1 | `early_lick_trials` in reference loader | Retain early trials because explicitly requested. |\n| Camera0 side `TongueTracking[:,1:3]` | `output[3]` tongue y | Match nearest 300-Hz frame to each bin center. Visible iff finite and DLC likelihood >=0.9; visible y is discretized with session-visible 40th/60th percentiles; invisible=3. | Method paper marker preprocessing | Confidence is sharply bimodal near 0/1, making 0.9 a stable visibility threshold. Threshold percentiles exclude invisible garbage coordinates. |\n| `subject.subject_id` | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | Sorted unique strings and per-session indices | — | 28 subjects expected. |\n| `units.anno_name` | `brain_regions`, `brain_region_idx` | Sorted unique detailed Allen CCF labels and per-neuron indices | `ccf_label` in reference preprocessing | Classifier-good units all have annotations. |\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Session/unit curation**: Retain only explicit classifier-good units. This yields 173 sessions automatically and follows the reference QC rather than inventing metric thresholds. Do not apply the method-paper’s analysis-specific <2-Hz cutoff.\n2. **Trial retention**: Retain every trial with exactly one go cue and all required streams; all 94,990 raw trials pass the one-go test, and the 173 neural-valid sessions contain 94,370. Requested categories would be destroyed by the reference “regular trial” mask. Auto/free-water trials remain because their task-table choice/outcome/early labels are defined; metadata will report them.\n3. **Choice definition**: Use outcome crossed with instruction. Across all raw trials this agrees with the first response-period lick for >99.6%; discrepancies are predominantly auto/free-water cases or sparse event-log glitches. It also guarantees the semantically required no-lick label for ignore trials.\n4. **Mixed output temporality**: Store all four outputs as `(4,80)` int arrays. Repeat per-trial choice/outcome/early labels across time and vary tongue class by bin. A single 2-D representation is required to combine per-trial and time-varying outputs in the validator/trainer.\n5. **Tongue cleaning/visibility**: Use DLC likelihood >=0.9, finite y, and nearest frame. The paper’s invisible-tongue mean imputation is deliberately not used because class 3 must preserve missing visibility. Percentiles are computed once per session over visible y samples. Five-SD velocity outliers will be inspected in sample plots; because the requested target is discretized and percentile-robust, any cleaning will be added only if it changes sampled-bin classes materially.\n6. **Binning/alignment**: Bin edges are exactly `np.linspace(-2.5,1.5,81)` and centers end at +1.475 s, avoiding an off-by-one endpoint. Spike counts use `[left,right)`, identical to reference logic, and are converted to Hz.\n7. **Time input semantics**: Follow the explicit Decoder Task (“time from tone onset in seconds, continuous, time-varying”) rather than the generic key-format suggestion that event times be binary.\n8. **Storage types**: Neural and inputs float32, outputs and region/subject indices compact integer types. This controls full-pickle size while preserving exact count-derived rates and labels.\n9. **Names/value order**: `input_names = ['time from tone onset (s)', 'photostimulation on']`; `output_names = ['lick direction choice','outcome','early lick','tongue y-position']`; values respectively `[['left','right','no lick'], ['ignore','miss','hit'], ['no','yes'], ['below 40th percentile','40th to 60th percentile','above 60th percentile','not visible']]`.\n10. **Metadata**: 50 ms, go-cue onset, offsets -2.5/+1.5 s, 80 centers, half-open bins, firing-rate units Hz, QC/trial retention, DLC threshold, percentile definition, event association, per-session thresholds/counts, and source paths/version.\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [ ] PyNWB-original neural spot check: independently count raw spikes for selected unit/trial/bin and compare converted Hz with `np.allclose`.\n- [ ] PyNWB-original input spot check: independently compute time-since-tone and laser-center membership for selected trial/bins and compare with `np.allclose`.\n- [ ] PyNWB-original output spot check: independently map trial table labels and nearest tongue frame/category using saved session thresholds; compare with `np.allclose`.\n- [ ] Verify every trial has one go and at least one sample onset; paired laser events; all extraction centers within available tongue timestamp range.\n- [ ] Verify 173 sessions, 28 subjects, 69,453 total session-neuron counts, and 94,370 retained trials unless a precisely logged data-quality exclusion is necessary.\n- [ ] Neural checks: nonnegative finite rates, multiples of 20 Hz, plausible mean firing rates, no unexpected all-zero session/unit.\n- [ ] Input checks: tone-time increases exactly 0.05 s/bin; Early licking replays the sample/delay epoch.\n- Spikes were Kilosort2 sorted. The method paper bins firing rates at 40-ms width/3.4-ms stride; requested downstream specification instead fixes 50-ms width and the -2.5-to-+1.5-s go-aligned interval.\n- Video/marker analysis uses side view, 300-Hz tracking. Reference marker cleaning detects velocity outliers at five SD and imputes from nearby frames. Occluded/in-mouth tongue positions were replaced by their mean for neural-prediction analyses. The requested decoder instead explicitly requires a `not visible` class, so visibility must be retained rather than mean-imputed.\n- Photoinhibition occurs in the last 0.5 s of delay and ends before go cue (including 100-ms ramp-down). This provides a strong timing sanity check for the binary photostimulation input.\n\n### Curation Steps\n\n**Neuron curation rules**:\nUse region-specific logistic-regression QC classifiers trained from manually curated Kilosort2 clusters using 15 quality metrics. “Good” classifier units were used in paper analyses. Cortex classifier covered cortex/hippocampus/olfactory/cortical subplate; striatum classifier covered striatum/pallidum; thalamus covered thalamus/hypothalamus; midbrain covered midbrain/pons; medulla covered medulla/cerebellum. QC ROC AUC averaged >0.9. The method paper additionally excluded <2-Hz neurons for its video-to-neural regression, but this is analysis-specific and not a global dataset curation rule.\n\n**Trial curation rules**:\nData-paper core analyses exclude early-lick and no-response trials and select high-performance sessions. Method-paper analyses exclude photoinhibition, free-water, early-lick, and ignore trials; behavioral prediction further uses only correct trials. These exclusions directly conflict with requested decoding of photostimulation, early lick, and ignore/no-lick outcomes, so retaining those trials is required unless a trial lacks the required alignment/data window. Auto/free-water trials require careful treatment because outcome/choice semantics may be non-volitional.\n\n### Decoders Trained\n| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n| Population lick choice | Logistic regression, 200-ms causal windows/10-ms step, nested 5-fold CV; the paper reports time-varying figure curves rather than a directly comparable single scalar. Chance = 0.5; strongest late-delay performance is in ALM. |\n| Behavioral choice from video | ROC AUC with 20-fold stratified CV on correct trials; figure curves, not a scalar directly comparable to this four-output decoder. |\n\nNo reference decoder predicts the requested three-way outcome, binary early lick, or four-way tongue-y class in the same formulation, so there is no paper accuracy directly comparable to those outputs.\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Discrepancies Found\n| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Papers say | Resolution |\n|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n| Session count | Preprocessing processes every raw session that has QC files | 174 NWBs; exactly one has no `good` classifier labels | 173 behavioral sessions | Exclude the zero-good-unit session naturally because it cannot yield a neural decoder session; remaining session count is exactly 173. |\n| Good-unit count | Classifier-QC IDs intersected with valid CCF/histology | 69,453 units labeled `classification='good'`; all have nonempty `anno_name` | 69,943 good units | Use the release’s explicit classifier labels, not reverse-engineered metric thresholds or the paper aggregate.
